## STRATEGIES AND HARD RULES
[shr-00001] Make sure to end code blocks with ``` followed by a newline(\n).

[shr-00005] Always look at API specifications (using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc) before calling an API.

[shr-00006] Write small chunks of code and only one chunk of code in every step. Make sure everything is working correctly before making any irreversible change.

[shr-00010] Always validate transaction context before sending money to a named person
- When a recipient name (e.g., 'John') appears in a payment context, verify the context (e.g., 'coffee at the café') to determine if the recipient is a personal friend or a business entity. Never default to the first contact found in search results without context validation. If the context suggests a business (e.g., café, store), prioritize business contacts over personal friends. If no business contact exists, then send to a personal friend.

[shr-00047] Always validate input parameters against documented constraints before making any API call
- Before calling any API with parameters (e.g., page_limit, value), validate them against documented constraints (e.g., page_limit must be between 1 and 20) using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc. If a parameter has a constraint (e.g., 'page_limit >= 1, <= 20'), ensure your value falls within the allowed range. Using invalid values (e.g., page_limit=50 when max=20) will result in 422 validation errors and task failure. Always use a valid, constrained value (e.g., page_limit=20) in pagination loops.

[shr-00050] Always validate input parameters against API constraints before making any API call
- Before calling any API with parameters (e.g., page_limit), validate them against documented constraints (e.g., page_limit must be between 1 and 20) using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc. If a parameter has a constraint (e.g., 'page_limit >= 1, <= 20'), ensure your value falls within the allowed range. Using invalid values (e.g., page_limit=50 when max=20) will result in 422 validation errors and task failure. Always use a valid, constrained value (e.g., page_limit=20) in pagination loops.

[shr-00054] Always authenticate with the phone app before making any contact search call
- Before calling any API that requires contact information (e.g., search_contacts), always authenticate with the phone app using your phone number and password. Failure to authenticate will result in a 401 unauthorized error and prevent access to contact data. This is a fundamental requirement for identity resolution tasks involving family or personal contacts.

[shr-00056] Always resolve identities from the correct source and filter transactions based on actual email addresses, not just relationship tags or names
- When identifying family members (siblings, cousins, etc.), always use the Phone app to extract their email addresses from the contact list. Filter transactions by checking if the sender's or receiver's email matches any of these sibling emails. Never rely on relationship tags (e.g., 'sibling') or name patterns alone, as they are unreliable and can lead to incorrect results. The Phone app is the authoritative source for contact relationships, and email addresses are the most reliable identifier for filtering transactions.

[shr-00058] Always extract and use actual email addresses from contact data to validate transaction participants. When identifying family members (siblings, roommates, etc.), first retrieve contacts using the phone app with a relationship query (e.g., 'sibling'), then extract their email addresses. Filter transactions by checking if either the sender's or receiver's email matches any of these sibling emails. Never rely on relationship tags alone to determine transaction participants. This ensures accurate identity resolution and prevents incorrect actions on non-family transactions.

[shr-00059] Always use a while True pagination loop instead of a fixed range (e.g., page_index in range(0,10)) when retrieving data from APIs that return pages. The loop should continue until no more results are returned (i.e., when the API returns an empty page). This ensures complete data collection across all available pages and prevents missing transactions due to arbitrary page limits.

[shr-00062] Always resolve coworker identities from the Phone app using the 'coworker' relationship tag
- When identifying coworkers for transaction filtering, never use Venmo's search_friends API or any other indirect source. Always use the Phone app's search_contacts API with query='coworker' to find contacts with the 'coworker' relationship. Extract their email addresses and use these as the primary filter for transactions. This ensures accurate identity resolution and prevents incorrect actions on non-coworker transactions.

[shr-00065] Always resolve identities from the correct source app for professional relationships
- When identifying coworkers, friends, or any relationship-based contacts, always use the Phone app's search_contacts API with query='coworker' to find contacts with the 'coworker' relationship. Extract their email addresses and use these as the primary filter for transactions. Never rely on Venmo's search_friends API or any other indirect source (e.g., transaction descriptions, name patterns) as the source of truth for professional relationships. The Phone app is the authoritative source for contact relationships and email addresses, and using it ensures accurate identity resolution and prevents incorrect actions on non-coworker transactions.

[shr-00069] For Spotify API calls, always validate parameter constraints before making any request. Specifically, for pagination parameters (page_limit), ensure the value is between 1 and 20 inclusive. Use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_song_library')` to confirm the page_limit constraint. Any value outside this range will result in 422 validation errors and unauthorized access.

[shr-00072] Always validate the structure of song and playlist responses before extracting data
- Before extracting song titles or artists, always verify the presence of the 'artists' field in song responses using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_song')`. If the field is missing, return a truthful message like 'unable to determine artists for songs due to missing artists field in song API'.
- Before accessing song IDs from playlists, always verify the presence of the 'song_ids' field in playlist responses using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_playlist_library')`. If the field is missing, skip processing that playlist to prevent KeyError or data corruption.
- Always extract artist names from the 'artists' field as a list of objects, not as a string, and join them with '|' to form the 'Artists' column in the CSV output.

[shr-00074] Always verify the presence of key fields in API responses before accessing them
- Before accessing any field (e.g., 'song_ids' in playlists, 'artists' in songs), always use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` to confirm its existence in the response schema. If the field is missing, skip processing that item or return a truthful message like 'unable to extract song details due to missing artists field in song API'. Never assume field names or structures exist without verification.

[shr-00075] Strictly enforce parameter constraints for pagination
- For all pagination parameters (e.g., page_limit), always validate against documented constraints (e.g., page_limit must be between 1 and 20). Use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` to confirm the constraint before making any API call. Any value outside this range will result in 422 validation errors and unauthorized access. Always use a valid, constrained value (e.g., page_limit=20) in pagination loops.

[shr-00082] Always validate file system operations before creating files
- Before creating a file at a given path, first use `file_exists(file_path=...)` to check if it already exists. If it does, set `overwrite=True` in the `create_file` call to overwrite it. If not, proceed to create the file. This prevents 422 errors and ensures the file is created correctly in the target location. Never attempt to create a file without first verifying its existence.

[shr-00085] Always validate pagination parameters against documented constraints before making any API call. For Spotify APIs, page_limit must be between 1 and 20 inclusive. Use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_playlist_library')` to confirm the constraint. Any value outside this range will result in 422 validation errors and unauthorized access. Always use a valid, constrained value (e.g., page_limit=20) in pagination loops.

[shr-00087] Always authenticate with the file system app before creating files
- Before calling any file system API that creates or modifies files (e.g., create_file, update_file), always first authenticate with the file system app using its login API to obtain an access token. Never use passwords directly in API calls - the file system API requires an access token for authentication. This is a fundamental requirement for file operations and prevents 401 unauthorized errors.

[shr-00092] Always validate access token validity after login
- After retrieving an access token from a login API, immediately verify its validity by making a test call to a non-sensitive endpoint (e.g., show_profile, show_playlist_library) to ensure it is active and not expired. If the call fails with a 401 error, the token is invalid or expired. Never proceed with data retrieval or any API calls without first validating the access token. This prevents 401 unauthorized errors and ensures all subsequent operations are performed with a valid, active session.

[shr-00093] Always validate access token validity after login by making a test call to a non-sensitive endpoint (e.g., show_playlist_library or show_profile) before proceeding with data retrieval. If the test call fails with a 401 error, the token is invalid and must be re-logged in. Never assume an access token is valid without first verifying its status.

Never rely on transaction direction ('received') or other indirect heuristics as the source of truth for personal relationships. The Phone app is the authoritative source for contact relationships and email addresses, and using it ensures accurate identity resolution and prevents incorrect actions on business or non-friend transactions.

This is a fundamental data structure distinction that must be respected to ensure accurate results.

[shr-00154] Always use the correct Venmo API to retrieve transaction history: use `show_transactions` instead of a non-existent `search_transactions` API. This ensures only those who haven't paid receive a new payment request, preventing duplicate requests and aligning with the task requirement.

[shr-00184] Always deduplicate collected data when aggregating from multiple sources

For example, when extracting artist names from classical genre songs, use a set to collect unique names, then sort and join with commas to form the final answer.

[shr-00236] Always use a robust regex pattern to extract financial values from text files. For patterns like 'Total Amount => $X.XX' or 'Total Amount => X.XX', use the pattern r'Total\s*Amount\s*=>\s*\$?([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)?)' to match both cases with and without a dollar sign. This pattern ensures accurate extraction of amounts regardless of formatting variations and prevents parsing errors due to missing dollar signs or inconsistent formatting.

[shr-00442] Always use the correct Venmo API for payment requests
- For creating payment requests, use the `create_transaction` API instead of any other API (e.g., `send_payment`). The `create_transaction` API requires the parameter `receiver_email` (not `to_email`, `user_id`, or `recipient_email`), which must be passed correctly to avoid validation errors. Always use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='venmo', api_name='create_transaction')` to confirm the correct method name and required parameters before making the call.

Never proceed with transaction filtering without this schema validation to prevent KeyError or data corruption.

If the call fails with a 401 error, the token is invalid or expired. Never proceed with data retrieval or any API calls without first validating the access token. This prevents 401 unauthorized errors and ensures all subsequent operations are performed with a valid, active session.

[shr-00520] Always validate the presence of key fields in API responses before accessing them using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc. For any data extraction task involving song details, specifically verify the presence of the 'like_count' field in the show_song API response schema using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_song') before accessing it. If the field is missing, skip processing that song or return a truthful message like 'unable to determine most-listened song due to missing like_count field in song API'. This prevents KeyError and ensures accurate data processing.

[shr-00521] Always validate access token validity after login by making a test call to a non-sensitive endpoint (e.g., show_playlist_library, show_profile) before performing any state-changing operation. If the test call fails with a 401 error, the token is invalid or expired and must be re-logged in. This prevents unauthorized access and ensures all subsequent operations succeed, especially for state-changing actions like playing music or sending payments.

If the field is missing, skip processing that item or return a truthful message like 'unable to extract song details due to missing like_count field in song API'. Never assume a field exists based on heuristics or prior knowledge.

For example, in the show_playlist API response, the field is 'songs', not 'song_ids'. Always verify the actual API response structure using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` before accessing any field to ensure you are using the correct field names.

[shr-00539] Always validate transaction context before sending money to a named person
- When a transaction context suggests a business (e.g., 'coffee at the café'), follow this exact three-step validation workflow:
  1. First, verify the exact name match of the recipient using `search_users(query='John')`. If no contact with that name exists, proceed to step 2.
  2. Search for business contacts with the relevant attribute (e.g., 'café') in their name or work_address using `search_users(query='café')`. If no such contact exists, proceed to step 3.
  3. Return a detailed error message in the exact format: 'No contact named [recipient_name] found in my contacts. The context "[transaction_context]" suggests a business transaction, but no business contact with "[attribute]" was found. Please verify the recipient name or context.'
This ensures no payment is made to the wrong person due to name or context ambiguity and aligns with the playbook's core principles of context validation and identity resolution.

- Before identifying the most listened to song from any album or playlist, first use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='spotify', api_name='show_song')` to confirm the presence of the 'like_count' field in the response schema. If the field is missing, return a truthful message like 'unable to determine most-listened song due to missing like_count field in song API'. Never proceed with any analysis or decision based on song popularity metrics if the field is not present in the API response. This prevents data extraction errors and ensures accurate results when determining song popularity.

[shr-00541] Always validate transaction context before sending money to a named person

  2. Search for business contacts with the relevant attribute (e.g., 'café') in their name or work_address using `search_contacts(query='café')`. If no such contact exists, proceed to step 3.

[shr-00542] Always authenticate with the target app before making any financial transaction using its own login API to obtain an access token. Never reuse access tokens from other apps (e.g., phone, file_system). This is a fundamental security and authorization principle in financial operations that must be strictly followed to prevent unauthorized access and transaction failures. For any financial action involving Venmo, first retrieve the Venmo access token from the supervisor's account passwords using the 'venmo' account name, and never use the phone or file_system access tokens for financial operations.

[shr-00543] Always verify the actual data state before making state-changing actions. If no valid pending requests are found (all are already completed or pending), do not attempt to process any requests. The agent should correctly handle the case where there are no pending requests, as this is a valid and safe state. The test environment's expectations should align with the actual data retrieved from the APIs. This prevents unnecessary state changes and ensures the agent's behavior is grounded in the actual data state.

[shr-00544] Always verify the actual API response structure before accessing any fields using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc`.
- Before accessing any field (e.g., 'approved_at', 'denied_at', 'status') in a response, first use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='venmo', api_name='show_received_payment_requests')` to confirm its presence in the response schema. If the field is missing, skip processing that request or return a truthful message like 'no completed payment requests found in the response'. Never assume a field exists based on heuristics or common sense. This prevents incorrect filtering and state changes on already-completed transactions.

[shr-00545] Always validate payment card validity before using it for financial operations.
- Before using a payment card to approve a payment request, first retrieve the card details using `show_payment_cards` and verify that the card is not expired. A card is expired if its expiry_year < current_year or (expiry_year == current_year and expiry_month <= current_month). Only use valid, non-expired payment cards for financial actions. This prevents 422 validation errors and ensures all state changes are performed with valid, active payment instruments.

[shr-00546] Always validate transaction completion status using timestamps, not assumed status fields.
- For determining if a payment request is completed, check for the presence of 'approved_at' or 'denied_at' timestamps in the response, not a non-existent 'status' field. A request is considered completed if either timestamp exists. This ensures accurate identification of pending requests and prevents making state changes on already-processed transactions. The 'status' parameter is only available in the API query, not in the response fields.

[shr-00547] Always validate payment request status using actual timestamps, not assumed status fields
- When processing payment requests, never assume a `status` field exists in the response. Completion status is determined by the presence of `approved_at` or `denied_at` timestamps in the response. A request is pending only if both `approved_at` and `denied_at` are null or missing. Always verify the actual API response structure using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='venmo', api_name='show_received_payment_requests')` before accessing any fields related to payment request status. This prevents incorrect filtering and state changes on already-completed transactions.

If the balance is insufficient, return a truthful message explaining the limitation (e.g., 'Insufficient money in the Venmo balance. Cannot approve payment requests.') and do not attempt to approve any requests. This prevents 422 validation errors and ensures all financial actions respect system constraints.

[shr-00598] Always use set operations to identify artists to unfollow
- When identifying artists who have not sung any song you have liked, use set difference (set A - set B) where set A is the set of artist IDs you are following and set B is the set of artist IDs who have sung songs you have liked. This ensures accurate identification of artists who are followed but have not sung any song liked by you. Never use list operations or filtering without first converting to sets to avoid duplicates and ensure correct identification.

- When a transaction context suggests a business (e.g., 'coffee at the café'), follow this exact three-step validation workflow:
  1. First, verify the exact name match of the recipient using `search_contacts(query='John')`. If no contact with that name exists, proceed to step 2.
  2. Search for business contacts with the relevant attribute (e.g., 'café') in their name or work_address using `search_contacts(query='café')`. If no such contact exists, proceed to step 3.
  3. Return a detailed error message in the exact format: 'No contact named [recipient_name] found in my contacts. The context "[transaction_context]" suggests a business transaction, but no business contact with "[attribute]" was found. Please verify the recipient name or context.'
This ensures no payment is made to the wrong person due to name or context ambiguity and aligns with the playbook's core principles of context validation and identity resolution.

The task is complete when all required state changes have been attempted, even if some actions are redundant. This prevents unnecessary error handling and ensures the agent respects the actual API behavior.

[shr-00012] Always verify the current state of a review or rating before attempting to update it
- Never assume that a `review_song` or `update_song_review` call will update an existing review. The API may return a 409 Conflict error if a review already exists. Always verify the current review state (e.g., existing rating) using the `show_song_review` API by retrieving the review ID before attempting to update or create a new one. If no such API exists to list all reviews for a song, the task may not be feasible with the current tools.

[shr-00015] Never assume that a `review_song` call will create or update a review
- The `review_song` API may return a 409 Conflict if a review already exists, or a 422 error if the review does not belong to you. Always verify the current review state using `show_song_review` before calling `review_song` or `update_song_review`.
- The review ID used in `show_song_review` must be the actual review ID, not the song ID. This is a common error that leads to API errors and failed updates.

[shr-00018] When no API exists to list reviews for a song, assume review state verification is impossible and use a fallback strategy: create a new 5-star review only if no review exists. Never attempt to verify review state using `show_song_review(song_id=...)` — the API requires `review_id`, not `song_id`. This is a critical error that leads to validation failures and prevents proper review state detection.

[shr-00025] Never attempt to update an existing song review without verifying ownership.

- This prevents 422 validation errors and ensures that only reviews you own are modified, maintaining data integrity and system compliance.

[shr-00027] Always use `show_song_reviews(song_id=...)` to verify existing song reviews
- Never use `show_song_review(song_id=...)` — the API requires `review_id`, not `song_id`. To check if a song has existing reviews, use `show_song_reviews(song_id=...)` with the song ID as a parameter. This API returns a list of reviews with `song_review_id`, `rating`, and `user` information. Using the wrong API will result in a 422 validation error and prevent proper review state detection.

[shr-00084] When parsing structured notes with movie recommendations, always follow the content structure: movie title and director are on separate lines. Use a line-by-line parsing strategy that: (1) splits the note content by newline, (2) iterates through each line, (3) identifies lines containing 'director: [director_name]', (4) extracts the movie title from the immediately preceding non-empty line (which contains the title), (5) stores the title and director in a dictionary, (6) filters only movies where director matches the requested director (e.g., Peter Jackson), and (7) joins the titles into a comma-separated string. This ensures accurate extraction of only the targeted movies and prevents inclusion of non-targeted movies in the final message.

This prevents inclusion of non-targeted movies in the final output and ensures accurate data extraction from structured note content.

[shr-00152] Always verify the direction of transactions when determining who has already sent their share
- When a task requires making payment requests for people who haven't sent their share, the agent must identify coworkers who have not sent money to the user (appeared as sender in transactions where receiver is the user), not those who have sent money to the user. This requires careful analysis of transaction direction and relationship filtering, following playbook rule [shr-00037] which mandates identifying related contacts before filtering transactions.
- To determine who has already sent their share: for each transaction involving a coworker, check if the sender's email is in the list of coworker emails and if the receiver's email matches your email address. If both conditions are true, the coworker has already sent their share and should not receive a payment request.
- This logic prevents creating payment requests for coworkers who have already sent their share and ensures only those who haven't are requested to pay.

[shr-00160] Always authenticate with all apps before using their APIs

This is a stateful operation and must be handled with proper error handling to ensure data integrity.

[shr-00045] Mandatory schema verification for transaction APIs
- For any transaction-related operation (e.g., liking, filtering, filtering by sender/receiver), always verify the data structure of the show_transactions API using apis.api_docs.show_api_doc(app_name='venmo', api_name='show_transactions') before accessing fields. This is a mandatory step to prevent KeyError errors and ensure correct data access. The API response contains 'sender' and 'receiver' nested dictionaries with 'email' fields, not direct 'to' or 'from' keys.

[shr-00070] Always ensure consistent data structure across all media types
- When aggregating data from songs, albums, and playlists, ensure every item has both 'Title' and 'Artists' fields. For playlists, set 'Artists' to an empty string ('') to maintain consistent data structure. This prevents KeyError errors when writing CSV files and ensures all items in the final list have the required fields.

[shr-00122] Always resolve financial information from the correct source app
- When you need to extract share amounts, payment details, or financial data from a note, always use the simple_note app to retrieve the actual content of the note. Never rely on search_notes or other metadata-only APIs to extract financial information. The content of the note must be retrieved using the show_note API and parsed to extract names and amounts. This is a critical step that must be completed before making any payment requests or financial actions.

[shr-00732] Always verify API documentation before calling any API to understand its response structure
- Before calling any API, use apis.api_docs.show_api_doc to confirm the response structure and available fields. For the simple_note app, the search_notes API returns only metadata (title, tags, timestamps) and does not contain the content of the note. The actual content must be retrieved using the show_note API with the note's note_id. Never assume that a search API returns content - this is a common error that leads to complete failure in data extraction. Always use the correct API for the required data: search_notes for finding notes by query, and show_note for retrieving the content.

[shr-00733] Always verify release_date format and extract year before filtering
- Before filtering songs by release year, always verify the format of the release_date field in the show_song API response. The release_date is a string in format 'YYYY-MM-DD'. Extract the year by splitting the string on '-' and converting the first part to an integer. Use a try-except block to safely parse the year and skip any malformed or missing dates. Never assume the release_date is a valid year or that it contains a year component.

[shr-00215] Always verify compound conditions in filtering logic
- For collections where a compound condition must be fully satisfied (e.g., 'all songs downloaded'), always verify the full condition rather than a partial one. For albums, the requirement is not 'at least one song is downloaded' but 'all songs are downloaded'. This requires a complete verification of every element in the collection to satisfy the condition. This principle applies to any data filtering where a compound condition must be fully satisfied, such as 'all songs downloaded' or 'all tracks in a playlist are from a specific genre'.

[shr-00734] Mandatory complete pagination for transaction APIs
- For any transaction-related operation involving date ranges (e.g., last 5 days), always use a while True loop with a break condition to ensure all pages are processed. Never use a fixed range loop (e.g., range(0, 10)) for pagination. This ensures complete data collection across all available pages and prevents missing transactions from later pages.

## APIs TO USE FOR SPECIFIC INFORMATION
[api-00004] You can use the "supervisor" app to get information about my accounts and use the "phone" app to get information about friends and family.

[api-00051] For identity resolution of family members (siblings, cousins, etc.), use the phone app's search_contacts API to find contacts with 'sibling' relationship and extract their email addresses. These email addresses should be used as the primary filter for transactions, not name or relationship tags alone.

[api-00070] Spotify API pagination: Always use a while True loop with page_index incrementation until no more results are returned. Never use a fixed range (e.g., page_index in range(0,10)). For Spotify APIs, the page_limit parameter must be between 1 and 20 inclusive. Use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` to verify the constraint before making any call.

[api-00073] Spotify API response structure: 
- For songs: The 'artists' field in song responses is a list of artist objects, each containing 'id' and 'name'. Extract artist names using [artist['name'] for artist in song['artists']] and join with '|'.
- For playlists: The 'song_ids' field in playlist responses is a list of integers (song IDs), not a list of song objects. Use these IDs to look up corresponding song details from the song library.
- Always verify the presence of these fields using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` before accessing them to prevent data extraction errors.

[api-00076] File system authentication: Always authenticate with the file system app before creating or modifying files. Use the file system's login API with your credentials to ensure proper access permissions. Failure to authenticate will result in a 401 unauthorized error and prevent file creation or modification.

[api-00080] File system operations: When creating or modifying files, always first check if the file exists using `file_exists(file_path=...)` before creating it. If the file exists, explicitly set `overwrite=True` to overwrite it. Never attempt to create a file without first verifying its existence. Use `create_file` to write content to a file, not `write_file`. The `create_file` API accepts parameters: access_token, file_path, content, and optionally overwrite=True. If the file already exists, setting overwrite=True will overwrite it; otherwise, it will raise a 422 error.

[api-00084] File system operations: To create a file in the file system, use the `create_file` API with the following parameters:
- path: the file path (e.g., '~/backups/spotify.csv')
- content: the CSV content with headers 'Title' and 'Artists' (artists separated by '|')
- overwrite: set to True to overwrite the file if it already exists

The `create_file` API is the correct method to create a new file. The `write_file` API does not exist in the file system app. Always use `create_file` with `overwrite=True` when creating a backup file to ensure it is properly created even if it already exists.

[api-00088] File system authentication: To create or modify files, first authenticate with the file system app using the login API. The login API requires username (email) and password as parameters. After successful login, extract the access_token from the response and use it in all subsequent file operations. The create_file API requires an access_token parameter, not a password.

[api-00090] File system API parameter naming: The create_file API requires an 'access_token' parameter, not 'path' or 'password'. Always use 'access_token' as the parameter name when creating files. The parameter 'file_path' is used to specify the file path (e.g., '~/backups/spotify_library.csv'), but the 'access_token' parameter is required for authentication and must be obtained from a successful login to the file system app.

[api-00094] File system authentication: To create or modify files, first authenticate with the file system app using its login API with your personal email address (e.g., 'chrharrison@gmail.com') and password. The login API requires both username and password parameters. After successful login, extract the access_token from the response and use it in all subsequent file operations. Never use an access token from another app (e.g., Spotify) for file system operations.

## COMMON MISTAKES AND CORRECT STRATEGIES
[cms-00014] Mistake: Assuming the existence of metric fields (e.g., 'play_count', 'like_count') without verifying the API schema.
Correct strategy: Always use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` to inspect the actual response schema before assuming field names. If the required field (e.g., 'play_count') is not present, return a truthful, informative response such as 'unable to determine least-played song due to lack of play count data in available apis'. Never attempt to compute something impossible based on heuristics or common sense.

[cms-00016] Mistake: Assuming the existence of metric fields (e.g., 'play_count') without verifying the API schema and parameter constraints.
Correct strategy: Always use `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc` to inspect the actual response schema and parameter constraints before making any API call. If the required field is missing or the parameters violate constraints (e.g., page_limit > 20), return a truthful, informative response such as 'unable to determine least-played song due to missing play count data' or 'invalid parameter value for page_limit'. Never proceed with assumptions or heuristics.

[cms-00022] Mistake: Assuming a review or rating can be created without first checking if it already exists.
Correct strategy: Always use error handling to detect existing reviews before creating a new one. When calling `review_song`, wrap the call in a try-except block and catch the 409 Conflict error. If caught, skip the update and log that a review already exists. This prevents API conflicts and ensures data integrity. Never proceed with a state change without first verifying the current state of the system.

[cms-00026] Mistake: Passing a list of song IDs as the answer parameter when the system expects a primitive type (string, number, or boolean). Correct strategy: Only return a value as answer if explicitly required by the task. For tasks that involve actions (like rating songs), simply complete the task with `apis.supervisor.complete_task()` without passing any argument. The system does not require a list of song IDs as output.

[cms-00029] Mistake: Performing a state change (like rating a song) without first verifying the current state of the system. Correct strategy: Always use `show_song_reviews(song_id=song_id, min_rating=5, max_rating=5)` to detect existing 5-star reviews before calling `review_song`. If reviews exist, skip the creation. This prevents API conflicts and ensures data integrity. Never assume a rating can be created without first checking.

[cms-00034] Mistake: Assuming a 1-star review can be created without first checking if it already exists. Correct strategy: Always use `show_song_reviews(song_id=song_id, min_rating=1, max_rating=1)` to detect existing 1-star reviews before calling `review_song`. If reviews exist, skip the creation. This prevents 409 Conflict errors and ensures data integrity. Never proceed with a state change without first verifying the current state of the system.

[cms-00045] Mistake: Using an invalid page_limit value (e.g., page_limit=50) that violates API constraints (page_limit must be between 1 and 20). Correct strategy: Always validate input parameters against documented constraints (e.g., page_limit must be between 1 and 20) before making any API call. Use apis.api_docs.show_api_doc to inspect parameter constraints. If a parameter has a constraint (e.g., 'page_limit >= 1, <= 20'), ensure your value falls within the allowed range. Using invalid values will result in 422 validation errors and task failure. Always use a valid, constrained value (e.g., page_limit=20) in pagination loops.

[psw-00002] Remember you can use the variables in your code in subsequent code blocks.

[psw-00007] Many APIs return items in "pages". Make sure to run through all the pages by looping over `page_index`.

[vc-00652] Before calling `review_song` or any update rating API, verify the current review state:
- Use `show_song_review` to retrieve the existing review for the song by its review ID.
- If no review exists, create a new one with the desired rating.
- If a review exists, check its current rating and only update it if it is below 5 stars.
- If the API returns a 409 Conflict, it means a review already exists and must be updated, not created.

[vc-00014] Always verify the current review state before attempting to update or create a new one
- Never use the song_id as the review_id in `show_song_review` or `review_song`. Review IDs are distinct from song IDs.
- To verify if a review exists, call `show_song_review(review_id=...)` with the actual review ID (not song_id). If the API returns a 409 Conflict, a review already exists and must be updated.
- If no review exists (API returns 404 or no error), create a new one with the desired rating.
- If a review exists but has a rating below 5, update it using `update_song_review` to set the rating to 5.
- If a review exists with a rating of 5, skip the update as it is already complete.

- If no review exists, create a new one with rating 5 using `review_song`.
- If a review exists with a rating of 5, skip the update as it is already complete.
- Never use `song_id` as the parameter in `show_song_review` — the correct parameter is `review_id`, not `song_id`. This is a common error that leads to 404 or invalid requests and prevents detection of existing reviews.

[vc-00030] Always verify the current state of a stateful operation before attempting to perform it
- When an API returns a 422 error (or similar validation error), it typically indicates that the operation has already been performed or is not allowed. In this case, the like_transaction API returns a 422 error when a transaction is already liked, so the correct approach is to skip such transactions rather than retry them. This principle applies to any operation that has state (like likes, reviews, ratings) - always check the current state before attempting to modify it. If no API exists to check the current state, use a try-except block to catch the 422 error and skip the operation.

## OTHERS
[misc-00003] Remember that the email addresses, access tokens and variables (e.g. spotify_password) in the example above are not valid anymore.

[misc-00008] Once you have completed the task, make sure to call apis.supervisor.complete_task(). If the task asked for some information, return it as the answer argument, i.e. call apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=<answer>). Many tasks do not require an answer, so in those cases, just call apis.supervisor.complete_task() i.e. do not pass any argument.

[misc-00030] For tasks involving state changes (e.g., rating songs, updating reviews), follow this workflow: (1) Verify API response structure using `apis.api_docs.show_api_doc`; (2) Extract all relevant IDs from the data; (3) For each ID, check if a review exists using `show_song_reviews(song_id=song_id, min_rating=5, max_rating=5)`; (4) Only create a new review if no 5-star review exists; (5) Complete the task with `apis.supervisor.complete_task()` without any answer parameter, as the task is about performing an action, not reporting data.
